## B13-0280 — How many major planets orbit the Sun in our solar system?

  Answer/context quote: “Our solar system has one star, eight planets” (8 words).

## B13-0282 — Which planet is closest to the Sun?

Answer: Mercury. Fun fact: Mercury is also the smallest of the eight major planets.

## B13-0283 — Which of the eight major planets is the smallest?

Answer: Mercury. Fun fact: Mercury completes an orbit of the Sun in about 88 Earth days.

  Answer/context quote: “Mercury is the smallest planet in our solar system” (9 words).

  Answer/context quote: “Mercury is the smallest planet (except for the dwarf planets)” (10 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “it has the shortest period of revolution about the Sun (88 of our days)” (14 words).

## B13-0284 — On which planet is one sidereal rotation longer than one orbit around the Sun?

Answer: Venus. Fun fact: Venus rotates in the opposite direction from Earth.

## B13-0287 — Which pair of planets are called the gas giants?

## B13-0305 — In lion prides, which adults do most of the hunting?

  Additional fun-fact quote: “They usually hunt in groups” (5 words).

## B13-0306 — Which animal is the fastest land mammal over a short sprint?

Answer: Cheetah. Fun fact: Its speed adaptations support very short sprints.

- animals-nature-s0005: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/cheetah)

  Answer/context quote: “Cheetahs are aerodynamically built for speed and are the fastest land mammal.” (12 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “No other land mammal surpasses their short sprints.” (8 words).

- animals-nature-s0055: [San Diego Zoo Animals & Plants](https://animals.sandiegozoo.org/animals/cheetah)

  Answer/context quote: “As the fastest land mammal, cheetahs have unique adaptations that help them run very fast for short distances.” (18 words).

## B13-0307 — Which cat has claws that remain partly exposed instead of retracting completely?

Answer: Cheetah. Fun fact: The claws help give it traction while running.

- animals-nature-s0005: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/cheetah)

  Answer/context quote: “Special paw pads and semi-retractable claws provide great traction.” (9 words).

- animals-nature-s0055: [San Diego Zoo Animals & Plants](https://animals.sandiegozoo.org/animals/cheetah)

  Answer/context quote: “A cheetah can retract its claws a bit” (8 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “Hard and sharp claws act like cleats, providing great traction when running.” (12 words).

## B13-0308 — A closely bonded group of adult male cheetahs is commonly called what?

Answer: A coalition. Fun fact: Brothers may remain together in one of these groups.

- animals-nature-s0005: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/cheetah)

  Answer/context quote: “A coalition is usually made up of two to three littermates and is a very tightly bonded group.” (18 words).

- animals-nature-s0055: [San Diego Zoo Animals & Plants](https://animals.sandiegozoo.org/animals/cheetah)

  Answer/context quote: “related males, usually brothers, live in small groups called coalitions.” (10 words).

## B13-0309 — Which cat’s black facial stripes are often nicknamed “tear marks”?

Answer: Cheetah. Fun fact: Cheetah cubs have a long-haired mantle along their backs.

- animals-nature-s0005: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/cheetah)

  Answer/context quote: “A black tear mark runs from the inner corner of each eye down to the mouth.” (16 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “Cubs are smoky-grey in color with long hair, called a mantle, running along their backs.” (15 words).

- animals-nature-s0055: [San Diego Zoo Animals & Plants](https://animals.sandiegozoo.org/animals/cheetah)

  Answer/context quote: “Cheetahs are the only cats with black “tear marks” on their face” (12 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “Cubs also have a stripe of long, silver fur called a mantle that runs down their back.” (17 words).

## B13-0310 — What is the characteristic dark coat pattern on a tiger?

Answer: Stripes. Fun fact: Tigers normally have pale or white undersides.

- animals-nature-s0007: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/tiger)

  Answer/context quote: “Tigers have reddish-orange coats with prominent black stripes, white bellies and white spots on their ears.” (16 words).

- animals-nature-s0008: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Panthera_tigris/)

  Answer/context quote: “vertical black stripes along the flanks and shoulders that vary in size, length, and spacing.” (15 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “The underside of the limbs and belly, chest, throat, and muzzle are white or light.” (15 words).

## B13-0311 — Outside mating and raising cubs, wild tigers generally live in which social pattern?

Answer: Solitary. Fun fact: A mother and her offspring are a major exception to that pattern.

- animals-nature-s0007: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/tiger)

  Answer/context quote: “Tigers in the wild are thought to be solitary creatures, except when mating or raising young.” (16 words).

- animals-nature-s0008: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Panthera_tigris/)

  Answer/context quote: “Tigers are solitary, the only long-term relationship is between a mother and her offspring.” (14 words).

## B13-0312 — Wild tigers are most active for hunting during which part of the day?

Answer: Night. Fun fact: They locate prey using sight and hearing.

- animals-nature-s0007: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/tiger)

  Answer/context quote: “These powerful cats hunt primarily at night, using sight and sound to identify prey.” (14 words).

- animals-nature-s0008: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Panthera_tigris/)

  Answer/context quote: “Tigers are most active at night” (6 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “Tigers are thought to locate their prey using hearing and sight more than olfaction” (14 words).

## B13-0313 — What color is the spot found on the back of a tiger’s ear?

Answer: White. Fun fact: Those pale ear marks contrast with the dark stripes on its body.

- animals-nature-s0007: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/tiger)

  Answer/context quote: “with prominent black stripes, white bellies and white spots on their ears.” (12 words).

- animals-nature-s0008: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Panthera_tigris/)

  Answer/context quote: “A white spot is present on the back of each ear.” (11 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “vertical black stripes along the flanks and shoulders” (8 words).

## B13-0314 — Meerkats belong to which animal family?

Answer: Mongoose. Fun fact: Their faces have conspicuous dark rings or patches around the eyes.

- animals-nature-s0009: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/meerkat)

  Answer/context quote: “A member of the mongoose family, meerkats” (7 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “dark patches around their eyes” (5 words).

- animals-nature-s0010: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Suricata_suricatta/)

  Answer/context quote: “Meerkats are one of the smallest species of mongooses in the family Herpestidae .” (14 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “Meerkats also have black rings around their large eyes.” (9 words).

## B13-0315 — What is a meerkat sentinel’s main role while its group forages?

Answer: Watches for danger. Fun fact: Members can take turns performing this lookout duty.

- animals-nature-s0009: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/meerkat)

  Answer/context quote: “One member of the group will act as a sentinel, on the lookout for predators.” (15 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “Individuals rotate sentinel duty throughout the day.” (7 words).

- animals-nature-s0010: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Suricata_suricatta/)

  Answer/context quote: “While mobs are foraging, one typically acts as sentinel and alarms others of potential dangers.” (15 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “This duty is shared between individuals within a mob” (9 words).

## B13-0316 — Which of these is a usual collective name for a group of meerkats?

Answer: A mob. Fun fact: Other group members can help parents care for young meerkats.

- animals-nature-s0009: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/meerkat)

  Answer/context quote: “A group of meerkats is called a mob.” (8 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “non-breeding individuals in the pack also help to care for the young.” (12 words).

- animals-nature-s0010: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Suricata_suricatta/)

  Answer/context quote: “They live in groups, called mobs or gangs.” (8 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “Other members of the social group, or mob, are also involved in rearing” (13 words).

## B13-0317 — Meerkats are described as diurnal. When are they normally active?

Answer: During the day. Fun fact: They spend much of their active time foraging.

- animals-nature-s0009: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/meerkat)

  Answer/context quote: “Meerkats are diurnal and spend most of their active time foraging, basking in the sun, and grooming.” (17 words).

- animals-nature-s0010: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Suricata_suricatta/)

  Answer/context quote: “Meerkats are diurnal and thus are frequently exposed to intense heat from the sun.” (14 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “Meerkats forage in groups for about 5 to 8 hours per day” (12 words).

## B13-0318 — A mature male gorilla is called a silverback because pale hair grows across which area?

Answer: Its back. Fun fact: The silverback name refers to age-related coat coloring.

- animals-nature-s0011: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/western-lowland-gorilla)

  Answer/context quote: “Mature silverback males have a saddle of white hair across the small of their backs.” (15 words).

- animals-nature-s0012: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Gorilla_gorilla/)

  Answer/context quote: “The dominant male, sometimes known as a silverback because of the age-related greying hair on his back” (17 words).

## B13-0319 — When a western gorilla walks on all fours, which part of its curled hand bears weight?

Answer: The knuckles. Fun fact: Its fingers are curled during this characteristic walk.

- animals-nature-s0011: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/western-lowland-gorilla)

  Answer/context quote: “when they move on all fours, they knuckle-walk, supporting their weight on the third and fourth digits of their curled hands.” (21 words).

- animals-nature-s0012: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Gorilla_gorilla/)

  Answer/context quote: “walk in a hunched, quadrupedal fashion, with hands curled and knuckles touching the ground.” (14 words).

## B13-0320 — Which food group supplies most of a wild western gorilla’s diet?

Answer: Plant material. Fun fact: Plant stems are among the foods it eats.

- animals-nature-s0011: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/western-lowland-gorilla)

  Answer/context quote: “Gorillas are primarily herbivorous, eating the leaves and stems of herbs, shrubs and vines.” (14 words).

- animals-nature-s0012: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Gorilla_gorilla/)

  Answer/context quote: “Wild gorillas are herbivores, subsisting mainly on juicy stemmed plants.” (10 words).

## B13-0321 — What materials do western gorillas arrange into their sleeping nests?

Answer: Branches and leaves. Fun fact: These nests may be built either on the ground or in trees.

- animals-nature-s0011: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/western-lowland-gorilla)

  Answer/context quote: “pulling down and tucking branches, leaves or other vegetation around themselves.” (11 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “Females may nest on the ground or in trees.” (9 words).

- animals-nature-s0012: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Gorilla_gorilla/)

  Answer/context quote: “Gorillas build day and night nests of branches and leaves for cushion on the ground or in trees.” (18 words).

## B13-0322 — What is the characteristic hair color of a Bornean orangutan?

Answer: Orange-red. Fun fact: Its long arms help it move through the tree canopy.

- animals-nature-s0013: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/orangutan)

  Answer/context quote: “Orangutans have long, sparse orange or reddish hair unequally distributed over their bodies.” (13 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “are very well adapted to life in the trees, with arms much longer than their legs.” (16 words).

- animals-nature-s0014: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Pongo_pygmaeus/)

  Answer/context quote: “Bornean orangutans have orange-red hair and long arms, which are advantageous for traveling through the canopy.” (16 words).

## B13-0323 — What name is used for the broad cheek pads of some adult male orangutans?

Answer: Flanges. Fun fact: Adult males can develop these conspicuous facial pads.

- animals-nature-s0013: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/orangutan)

  Answer/context quote: “Flanged males (males with flanges, also known as cheek pads)” (10 words).

- animals-nature-s0014: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Pongo_pygmaeus/)

  Answer/context quote: “Males also develop large cheek pads known as flanges” (9 words).

## B13-0324 — Where do Bornean orangutans normally construct their sleeping nests?

Answer: In trees. Fun fact: Their hair is characteristically orange or reddish.

- animals-nature-s0013: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/orangutan)

  Answer/context quote: “Orangutans typically build sleeping nests above the ground in various positions of one or more trees.” (16 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “Orangutans have long, sparse orange or reddish hair” (8 words).

- animals-nature-s0014: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Pongo_pygmaeus/)

  Answer/context quote: “Bornean orangutans sleep in nest platforms made of vegetation 40 to 60 feet off the ground.” (16 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “Bornean orangutans have orange-red hair” (5 words).

## B13-0325 — Which food is the main component of a Bornean orangutan’s diet?

Answer: Fruit. Fun fact: By eating fruit, orangutans help disperse seeds.

- animals-nature-s0013: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/orangutan)

  Answer/context quote: “Orangutans eat primarily fruit and play an important role dispersing seeds through defecation.” (13 words).

- animals-nature-s0014: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Pongo_pygmaeus/)

  Answer/context quote: “Bornean orangutans are frugivorous” (4 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “they play a vital role in seed dispersal” (8 words).

## B13-0326 — How many fingerlike projections are at the tip of an Asian elephant’s trunk?

Answer: One. Fun fact: Elephant tusks are modified teeth of the upper jaw.

- animals-nature-s0015: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/asian-elephant)

  Answer/context quote: “Asian elephants have one small projection at the end of their trunk” (12 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “Tusks are modified upper incisors” (5 words).

- animals-nature-s0016: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Elephas_maximus/)

  Answer/context quote: “Asian elephants have one fingerlike projection at the tip of the trunk” (12 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “Males have tusks which are an elongation of the second upper incisors” (12 words).

## B13-0327 — Elephant tusks are elongated examples of which kind of tooth?

Answer: Incisors. Fun fact: They are modified teeth of the upper jaw.

  Answer/context quote: “Tusks are modified upper incisors that grow throughout an individual's life” (11 words).

- animals-nature-s0016: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Elephas_maximus/)

  Answer/context quote: “Males have tusks which are an elongation of the second upper incisors” (12 words).

## B13-0328 — An Asian elephant’s wild diet is primarily composed of what?

Answer: Plants. Fun fact: Leaves are among its foods.

- animals-nature-s0015: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/asian-elephant)

  Answer/context quote: “Asian elephants are herbivores” (4 words).

## B13-0329 — Which listed animal typically has larger adult males than females?

Answer: Asian elephant. Fun fact: The species shows a noticeable size difference between the sexes.

Answer: Bamboo. Fun fact: Red pandas chiefly eat its leaves.

## B13-0332 — What pattern is visible on a red panda’s tail?

Answer: Alternating red and buff rings. Fun fact: Its tail is long and furry.

## B13-0334 — What supplies the pigments responsible for an American flamingo’s pink plumage?

Answer: Its food. Fun fact: Dietary pigments become incorporated into growing feathers.

- animals-nature-s0023: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/american-flamingo)

  Answer/context quote: “A pigment in their food gives their feathers their flamboyant pink color.” (12 words).

- animals-nature-s0024: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Phoenicopterus_ruber/)

## B13-0335 — What color are an adult American flamingo’s flight feathers?

Answer: Black. Fun fact: These dark feathers contrast with its pink body plumage.

- animals-nature-s0023: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/american-flamingo)

  Answer/context quote: “A flamingo's plumage is a distinctive pink color with black flight feathers along the edges of the wings.” (18 words).

- animals-nature-s0024: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Phoenicopterus_ruber/)

## B13-0336 — What general shape does an American flamingo’s nest have?

Answer: A raised mound. Fun fact: Both partners help build the nest.

- animals-nature-s0023: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/american-flamingo)

  Answer/context quote: “both parents help build a volcano-shaped nest” (7 words).

  Answer/context quote: “they lay a single egg that they then incubate” (9 words).

## B13-0338 — Which species is the largest living bird?

Answer: Ostrich. Fun fact: Its native range includes Africa.

## B13-0341 — The common ostrich’s surviving native wild range is on which continent?

## B13-0344 — Which listed species has females that generally outweigh males?

## B13-0345 — Emus belong to the ratites, a group best known for which trait?

Answer: Being flightless. Fun fact: Their long legs resemble those of other running ratites.

  Answer/context quote: “Emus are large, flightless birds resembling and related to ostriches.” (10 words).

- animals-nature-s0028: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Dromaius_novaehollandiae/)

  Answer/context quote: “Emus have long bare legs, similar to other flightless birds Ratites .” (12 words).

## B13-0346 — What is the helmet-like structure on a southern cassowary’s head called?

Answer: A casque. Fun fact: The structure is on top of the head.

- animals-nature-s0029: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/southern-cassowary)

  Answer/context quote: “One of the cassowary’s most unique and dinosaur-like features is the casque atop its head.” (15 words).

- animals-nature-s0030: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Casuarius_casuarius/)

  Answer/context quote: “On their heads there is a large bony casque which is made of trabecular bone and cartilage.” (17 words).

## B13-0347 — What is the main food in a southern cassowary’s diet?

Answer: Fruit. Fun fact: It eats fruit in its forest habitat.

  Answer/context quote: “Southern cassowaries are frugivorous, feeding mostly on fruits from canopy species in the forests where they live.” (17 words).

## B13-0348 — How are southern cassowary eggs normally incubated?

Answer: The male broods them. Fun fact: Female cassowaries are generally larger than males.

- animals-nature-s0029: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/southern-cassowary)

  Answer/context quote: “Males are the sole caregivers, incubating the eggs by themselves and raising the chicks” (14 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “Females are slightly larger than males” (6 words).

- animals-nature-s0030: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Casuarius_casuarius/)

  Answer/context quote: “Incubation, which is exclusively done by the males” (8 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “Females are 127 to 170 cm long and up to 59 kg, are larger than the males” (17 words).

## B13-0349 — Female-biased adult body size is characteristic of which animal here?

Answer: Southern cassowary. Fun fact: Males incubate the eggs.

- animals-nature-s0029: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/southern-cassowary)

  Answer/context quote: “Females are slightly larger than males” (6 words).

  Answer/context quote: “Females are 127 to 170 cm long and up to 59 kg, are larger than the males” (17 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “Incubation, which is exclusively done by the males” (8 words).

## B13-0350 — Which insects are a staple food for sloth bears?

Answer: Termites. Fun fact: Their strong claws open termite nests.

- animals-nature-s0019: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/sloth-bear)

  Answer/context quote: “Termites, dug out of their cement-hard nest mounds, and some ants are year-round staples.” (14 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “long, curved claws which they use to excavate termites and ants.” (11 words).

- animals-nature-s0020: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Melursus_ursinus/)

  Answer/context quote: “feast on their prize by vacuuming the termites into their mouths.” (11 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “rip open the nest with their long claws” (8 words).

## B13-0351 — What can a sloth bear close to keep out dust while raiding an insect nest?

- animals-nature-s0020: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Melursus_ursinus/)

  Answer/context quote: “The ability to voluntarily open and close the nostrils prevents the inhalation of dust during this process.” (17 words).

  Answer/context quote: “secretions from scent glands are rubbed onto the tail, then wafted at opposing animals.” (14 words).

## B13-0385 — Which sex typically dominates a ring-tailed lemur troop’s hierarchy?

Answer: Females. Fun fact: Females typically remain in their birth group.

  Answer/context quote: “Females are dominant within groups, meaning females have preferential access to food” (12 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “Females spend their whole lives in their birth group.” (9 words).

- animals-nature-s0044: [Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Lemur_catta/)

  Answer/context quote: “All females are dominant over all males.” (7 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “Females stay with the same troop they were born into” (10 words).

## B13-0386 — What is the characteristic coat color of a golden lion tamarin?

Answer: Reddish-gold. Fun fact: A mane frames its face and head.

## B13-0390 — Why is the Asian small-clawed otter aptly named?

Answer: Its claws stay short of the toe pads. Fun fact: Its toes are webbed.

- animals-nature-s0047: [Smithsonian's National Zoo and Conservation Biology Institute](https://nationalzoo.si.edu/animals/asian-small-clawed-otter)

- animals-nature-s0058: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_small-clawed_otter)

  Answer/context quote: “It has short claws that do not extend beyond the pads of its webbed digits.” (15 words).

## B13-0391 — What is notable about the webbing between an Asian small-clawed otter’s toes?

Answer: It is incomplete. Fun fact: The webbing stops before the ends of the digits.

- animals-nature-s0058: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_small-clawed_otter)

  Additional fun-fact quote: “the spice made of its seed” (6 words).

- food-everyday-life-s0010: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nutmeg)

  Answer/context quote: “nutmeg, from its seed, and mace , from the seed covering” (11 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “nutmeg, from its seed, and mace , from the seed covering” (11 words).

## B13-0906 — Which pollen-receiving flower parts are harvested to make saffron?

Answer: Stigmas. Fun fact: The pigment crocin helps give saffron its golden-yellow coloring.

- food-everyday-life-s0011: [Encyclopaedia Britannica](https://www.britannica.com/plant/saffron)

  Answer/context quote: “golden-colored pungent stigmas (pollen-receiving structures)” (5 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “The coloring matter is crocin” (5 words).

- food-everyday-life-s0012: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saffron)

  Answer/context quote: “The vivid crimson stigma and styles (in industry jargon: 'threads') are collected and dried” (14 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “a carotenoid pigment, crocin , which imparts a rich golden-yellow hue” (11 words).

## B13-0907 — Which compound chiefly gives black pepper its pungency?

Answer: Piperine. Fun fact: Whole black peppercorns are dried fruits of the pepper vine.

- food-everyday-life-s0013: [Encyclopaedia Britannica](https://www.britannica.com/plant/black-pepper-plant)

  Answer/context quote: “The characteristic flavor is principally derived from the chemical piperine” (10 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “The fruits, which are sometimes called peppercorns” (7 words).

- food-everyday-life-s0014: [Wikipedia contributors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_pepper)

  Answer/context quote: “Its spiciness is due to the chemical compound piperine” (9 words).

  Additional fun-fact quote: “cultivated for its fruit (the peppercorn ), which is usually dried and used as a spice” (16 words).
